+<!-- brief extracted from WS-03-deploy-secrets-backups.md -->
+### Task WS-03.19: LLM provider env contract: Bedrock only in strict, the fake-LLM endpoint only in dev and test, no static AWS keys
+**Covers:** none (the IF-02 half of D-04's Bedrock routing; the provider code and the LLM register entries stay with WS-09)
+**Severity:** high
+**Wave:** 1
+**Depends on:** WS-03.6, WS-09.18
+**Implementer model:** opus
+**Review:** security-critical
+**Decision gate:** D-04 (changed 2026-10-19: real-user LLM traffic goes to Anthropic models on AWS Bedrock under the AWS BAA. Strict environments require `LLM_PROVIDER=bedrock`; `BEDROCK_MODEL_ID` has no default and is set at deploy after checking availability with `bedrock:ListFoundationModels` and the inference profiles, never guessed in code or tests, and it is required in strict once `LLM_PHI_EGRESS_APPROVED=true` (WS-09.4 makes approved-without-model-id a startup error), so a strict stack before the AWS BAA starts with the LLM off and needs no placeholder; `LLM_PROVIDER=portkey` stays allowed only in development and test with synthetic data; `BEDROCK_REGION` defaults to `us-east-1`, `BEDROCK_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` to `20`; `BEDROCK_ENDPOINT_URL` exists only for test and E2E against the fake LLM and is refused in strict; credentials come from the host's instance role (WS-03.20), never static AWS keys in env or secrets; LLM features stay off for real users until the owner accepts the AWS BAA in AWS Artifact and it is recorded; caching off, no semantic cache, memory's OpenAI embeddings and Weaviate writes off, unchanged)
+**Real infra:** no (renders `docker compose config` only)
+**Cross-workstream prerequisites:** WS-09.18 (the Bedrock provider in `UnifiedLLMClient`) reads exactly these names, takes credentials from the default boto3 chain and refuses `BEDROCK_ENDPOINT_URL` and `LLM_PROVIDER=portkey` in strict (requested below; this task depends on it); WS-05.1's fake LLM serves `POST /model/{model_id}/converse`, and WS-05.21 sets the e2e values, the endpoint only in the dev-only fragment `infrastructure/test/compose/llm-dev.yml` (WS-05 request 19); WS-01.2 pins `boto3` and `botocore` in the runtime lock (D-04).
+**Files:**
+- Modify: `deploy/env/registry.py` (`LLM_SERVICES: tuple[str, ...]`, the five services that call the LLM: orchestrator, safety, therapy, diagnosis and personality; `DENIED_NAMES: frozenset[str]`: `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `AWS_SESSION_TOKEN` and `AWS_PROFILE`, which no registry row may name and no rendered container may receive)
+- Modify: `deploy/env/env-contract.yaml` (rows on the five LLM services: `LLM_PROVIDER` (dev default `portkey`; strict the compose literal `bedrock`), `BEDROCK_REGION` (default `us-east-1`), `BEDROCK_MODEL_ID` (`required_in_strict: false` for the render, no dev or strict default, `example` the placeholder `REPLACE_AT_DEPLOY_FROM_LIST_FOUNDATION_MODELS`, which is not a model id; notes: chosen at deploy after checking availability with `bedrock:ListFoundationModels` and the inference profiles; required in strict once `LLM_PHI_EGRESS_APPROVED=true`, enforced at startup by WS-09.4), `BEDROCK_ENDPOINT_URL` (profiles `[dev]`, dev default empty; test and E2E point it at the fake LLM), `BEDROCK_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (default `20`); `LLM_PORTKEY_API_KEY`, `LLM_PORTKEY_GATEWAY_URL` and `LLM_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` get profiles `[dev]`, because Portkey and the direct Anthropic API are development and test paths with synthetic data only; `LLM_ENABLE_CACHING=false` and `LLM_PHI_EGRESS_APPROVED=false` unchanged), `.env.example` (RENDER)
+- Modify: `docker-compose.yml` (the five LLM services: `LLM_PROVIDER: ${LLM_PROVIDER:-portkey}`, `BEDROCK_REGION: ${BEDROCK_REGION:-us-east-1}`, `BEDROCK_MODEL_ID: ${BEDROCK_MODEL_ID:-}`, `BEDROCK_ENDPOINT_URL: ${BEDROCK_ENDPOINT_URL:-}`, `BEDROCK_TIMEOUT_SECONDS: ${BEDROCK_TIMEOUT_SECONDS:-20}`)
+- Modify: `docker-compose.prod.yml` (the five: `LLM_PROVIDER: bedrock` as a literal, so no operator value can select Portkey; `BEDROCK_MODEL_ID: ${BEDROCK_MODEL_ID:-}` (empty keeps the LLM off; with `LLM_PHI_EGRESS_APPROVED=true` an empty value fails startup in WS-09.4); `BEDROCK_ENDPOINT_URL`, `LLM_PORTKEY_API_KEY`, `LLM_PORTKEY_GATEWAY_URL` and `LLM_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` removed with `!reset null`)
+- Modify: `tests/deploy/test_env_contract.py` (`test_registry_entries_are_well_formed` also fails on any row whose `name` or `source` is in `DENIED_NAMES`)
+- Test: `tests/deploy/test_d04_llm_provider_env.py`
+**Interfaces:**
+- Consumes: the `render` and `registry` fixtures and `ComposeRenderError` (WS-03.1).
+- Produces: the IF-02 names `LLM_PROVIDER`, `BEDROCK_REGION`, `BEDROCK_MODEL_ID`, `BEDROCK_ENDPOINT_URL` and `BEDROCK_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` on the five LLM services, consumed by WS-09.18 and WS-05.21 (New interfaces); `deploy.env.registry.LLM_SERVICES` and `DENIED_NAMES`; the strict rules (Bedrock only, a deploy-time model id, no endpoint override, no Portkey or direct Anthropic key, no static AWS credentials) that WS-03.20's instance role completes. This lands WS-09's CR-14 for WS-09.18 and the LLM-provider half of CR-4 for WS-09.4 and WS-09.5 (WS-03.6's generic W1 sync leaves these rows to this task), and answers WS-05's request 19 for WS-05.21: `BEDROCK_ENDPOINT_URL` is a dev-profile row, so the dev render with WS-05.21's fragment is accepted, and the strict render never carries it, so `test_env_contract.py` has no allow-listed strict difference for it. WS-05.21 depends on this task.

+- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test** in `tests/deploy/test_d04_llm_provider_env.py`
+```python
+"""D-04: strict LLM traffic goes only to Bedrock with a deploy-time model id; the fake-LLM endpoint and the Portkey and
+direct Anthropic keys never reach a strict container; no container is given static AWS credentials."""
+import re
+import pytest
+from deploy.env.registry import DENIED_NAMES, LLM_SERVICES
+from tests.deploy.conftest import REPO
+
+STATIC_AWS = {"AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID", "AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY", "AWS_SESSION_TOKEN", "AWS_PROFILE"}
+DEV_ONLY = ("BEDROCK_ENDPOINT_URL", "LLM_PORTKEY_API_KEY", "LLM_PORTKEY_GATEWAY_URL", "LLM_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY")
+
+def test_llm_services_and_denied_names():
+    assert set(LLM_SERVICES) == {"orchestrator-service", "safety-service", "therapy-service",
+                                 "diagnosis-service", "personality-service"}
+    assert STATIC_AWS <= set(DENIED_NAMES)
+
+@pytest.mark.parametrize("environment", ["staging", "production"])
+def test_strict_routes_every_llm_service_to_bedrock(render, environment):
+    r = render("strict", environment=environment,
+               overrides={"LLM_PROVIDER": "portkey", "BEDROCK_ENDPOINT_URL": "http://fake-llm:8080",
+                          "BEDROCK_MODEL_ID": "synthetic-model-id-not-real"})
+    for svc in LLM_SERVICES:
+        env = r.services[svc]["environment"]
+        assert env["LLM_PROVIDER"] == "bedrock", svc
+        assert env["BEDROCK_MODEL_ID"] == "synthetic-model-id-not-real", svc
+        assert env["BEDROCK_REGION"] == "us-east-1" and env["BEDROCK_TIMEOUT_SECONDS"] == "20", svc
+        assert not [k for n in DEV_ONLY for k in (n, f"{n}_FILE") if env.get(k)], svc
+
+def test_strict_render_without_a_model_id_keeps_the_llm_off(render):
+    r = render("strict", drop=["BEDROCK_MODEL_ID"])
+    for svc in LLM_SERVICES:
+        env = r.services[svc]["environment"]
+        assert (env["BEDROCK_MODEL_ID"], env["LLM_PHI_EGRESS_APPROVED"]) == ("", "false"), svc
+
+def test_dev_can_point_bedrock_at_the_fake_llm(render):
+    r = render("dev", environment="development",
+               overrides={"LLM_PROVIDER": "bedrock", "BEDROCK_ENDPOINT_URL": "http://fake-llm:8080"})
+    for svc in LLM_SERVICES:
+        env = r.services[svc]["environment"]
+        assert (env["LLM_PROVIDER"], env["BEDROCK_ENDPOINT_URL"]) == ("bedrock", "http://fake-llm:8080"), svc
+
+def test_dev_defaults_keep_portkey_and_no_endpoint(render):
+    env = render("dev", environment="development",
+                 drop=["LLM_PROVIDER", "BEDROCK_ENDPOINT_URL"]).services["therapy-service"]["environment"]
+    assert env["LLM_PROVIDER"] == "portkey" and env["BEDROCK_ENDPOINT_URL"] == ""
+
+@pytest.mark.parametrize("profile", ["dev", "strict"])
+def test_no_container_is_given_static_aws_credentials(render, registry, profile):
+    r = render(profile, overrides={n: "synthetic-not-a-key" for n in STATIC_AWS})
+    leaked = {s: sorted(STATIC_AWS & set(d.get("environment") or {})) for s, d in r.services.items()}
+    assert {s: v for s, v in leaked.items() if v} == {}
+    assert not STATIC_AWS & ({v.name for v in registry} | {v.source for v in registry})
+    example = (REPO / ".env.example").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
+    assert [n for n in STATIC_AWS if re.search(rf"^{n}=", example, re.M)] == []
+```

Collection fails with `ImportError: cannot import name 'DENIED_NAMES'` (and `LLM_SERVICES`) from `deploy.env.registry`; with those two constants added, `test_strict_routes_every_llm_service_to_bedrock` fails with `KeyError: 'LLM_PROVIDER'` for `orchestrator-service`, because nothing selects a provider and every LLM service still reaches Portkey through the `LLM_PORTKEY_*` rows in strict too (D-04 changed the strict path to Bedrock), `test_strict_render_without_a_model_id_keeps_the_llm_off` fails with `KeyError: 'BEDROCK_MODEL_ID'`, and the two dev tests fail with `KeyError` for the same names. `test_no_container_is_given_static_aws_credentials` passes today and must stay green.
+- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** the registry constants and rows and the compose edits listed under Files. Approach: the strict overlay writes `LLM_PROVIDER: bedrock` as a literal and removes the dev-only names with `!reset null` (Compose 2.24+, the same feature WS-03.3 uses for `ports: !reset []`); if the pinned Compose keeps an environment key under `!reset null`, give those rows a strict row with the empty literal instead, because the test checks the rendered result, not the mechanism. From W3, WS-03.7's `!override` of each service's `secrets` list keeps the dev-only key files out of strict. `test_registry_entries_are_well_formed` is extended to fail on any row named in `DENIED_NAMES`. The `BEDROCK_MODEL_ID` example in `.env.example` is deliberately not a model id, so a strict host that copies it fails at its first Bedrock call instead of reaching an unchosen model; an empty value keeps the LLM off until approval, when WS-09.4 makes it a startup error; no real model id appears anywhere in the tree. Finish with RENDER.

+<!-- brief extracted from WS-03-deploy-secrets-backups.md -->
+### Task WS-03.6: Notification delivery env contract, the crisis acknowledgement route and the W1/W2 env sync
+**Covers:** MVP-042
+**Severity:** high
+**Wave:** 1
+**Depends on:** WS-03.5
+**Implementer model:** opus
+**Review:** safety-critical
+**Decision gate:** D-12 (accepted 2026-10-19: Amazon SES in the pilot Region through its SMTP interface with STARTTLS, `EMAIL_SMTP_TLS_MODE=starttls`; SMS optional for CRITICAL; push out of scope; from W3 the SES SMTP username and password are file secrets from the environment's SOPS file (WS-03.8, D-07), and verifying the SES domain identity is an owner action (WS-03.20, D-25)), D-28 (default assumed: notification-service is the only pager and owns the acknowledgement link)
+**Real infra:** yes (`caddy adapt` for the acknowledgement route; BOOTSMOKE)
+**Cross-workstream prerequisites:** WS-13 reads `EMAIL_SMTP_TLS_MODE`, `NOTIFICATION_ACK_BASE_URL` and the coalescing/re-page settings in W1 phase B (IF-09); WS-04.7 consumes the Alertmanager rows and the Prometheus mount. This task also lands every env, route and service request that other plans address to WS-03 for wave 1 or wave 2 (WS-03 has no W2 wave).
+**From the W0 bug round:** C-5: the notification delivery settings (SMTP host, port, TLS mode, sender, fallback mailbox) are not part of the env contract, so a strict boot can start with an alert channel that cannot send. Registry rows and a strict check are in this task's scope; WS-13.7 owns the TLS mode and the single-attempt behaviour. (The bug-round hunter text was not preserved; this line is rebuilt from the 2026-11-03 ledger summary and this task's own scope. Re-confirm the failure in a RED test before fixing.)
+**Files:**
+- Modify: `docker-compose.yml` (notification-service: `EMAIL_ENABLED: ${EMAIL_ENABLED:-true}`, `EMAIL_SMTP_HOST: ${EMAIL_SMTP_HOST:-mailhog}`, `EMAIL_SMTP_PORT: ${EMAIL_SMTP_PORT:-1025}`, `EMAIL_SMTP_USERNAME`, `EMAIL_SMTP_PASSWORD`, `EMAIL_SMTP_TLS_MODE: ${EMAIL_SMTP_TLS_MODE:-none}`, `EMAIL_FROM_EMAIL: ${EMAIL_FROM_EMAIL:-alerts@localhost.invalid}`, `EMAIL_FROM_NAME: ${EMAIL_FROM_NAME:-Solace-AI}`, `NOTIFICATION_FALLBACK_ONCALL_EMAIL: ${NOTIFICATION_FALLBACK_ONCALL_EMAIL:-fallback-oncall@localhost.invalid}`, `NOTIFICATION_ALERT_COALESCE_SECONDS: "900"`, `NOTIFICATION_REPAGE_AFTER_SECONDS: "900"`, `NOTIFICATION_MAX_REPAGES: "3"`, `NOTIFICATION_ACK_BASE_URL: ${NOTIFICATION_ACK_BASE_URL:-http://api.localhost:8088}`, `SMS_ENABLED: ${SMS_ENABLED:-false}` and the `SMS_*` names the code reads, `PUSH_ENABLED: "false"`; the misleading comment about setting `EMAIL_SMTP_HOST` through `.env.prod` is removed; prometheus mounts `./infrastructure/prometheus:/etc/prometheus:ro` instead of the single file)
+- Modify: `docker-compose.prod.yml` (notification-service: `EMAIL_SMTP_HOST`, `EMAIL_FROM_EMAIL`, `EMAIL_SMTP_USERNAME`, `EMAIL_SMTP_PASSWORD`, `NOTIFICATION_FALLBACK_ONCALL_EMAIL` are `:?`; `EMAIL_SMTP_PORT: ${EMAIL_SMTP_PORT:-587}`; `EMAIL_SMTP_TLS_MODE: ${EMAIL_SMTP_TLS_MODE:-starttls}`; `NOTIFICATION_ACK_BASE_URL: https://api.${DOMAIN:?...}`)
+- Modify: `Caddyfile` (inside the API site's `route`, after `never_routed`: `handle /api/v1/notifications/crisis-alerts/* { reverse_proxy notification-service:8003 { health_uri /live health_interval 10s health_fails 3 lb_try_duration 10s } }` ahead of the orchestrator proxy)
+- Modify: `deploy/env/env-contract.yaml` (the notification rows, with the strict `example` for `EMAIL_SMTP_HOST` set to `email-smtp.us-east-1.amazonaws.com`, the SES SMTP endpoint of the pilot Region (D-12); `SMTP_HOST`, `SMTP_PORT`, `EMAIL_FROM`, `TWILIO_*`, `FCM_*` interpolation names dropped; the six `ALERTMANAGER_*` rows with services `[alertmanager]`, `ALERTMANAGER_SMTP_PASSWORD` secret, `ALERTMANAGER_EMAIL_TO` and `ALERTMANAGER_SMTP_SMARTHOST` required in strict, `ALERTMANAGER_SMTP_REQUIRE_TLS` strict default `true`), `.env.example` (RENDER)
+- Modify: `tests/deploy/test_env_contract.py` (the strict renders also run with `fragments=["docker-compose.observability.yml"]` when that file exists)

+- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test** in `tests/deploy/test_mvp_042_notification_env.py`
+```python
+"""MVP-042: strict notification-service gets a real SMTP relay with TLS and a fallback mailbox, never the dev mailhog;
+flags are overridable; .env.example names what the code reads; clinicians' acknowledgement links route to notification."""
+import json, re
+import pytest
+from deploy.env.compose_render import ComposeRenderError
+from deploy.env.registry import APP_SERVICES
+from tests.deploy.conftest import REPO
+
+def _notif(r):
+    return r.services["notification-service"]["environment"]
+
+def test_strict_smtp_comes_from_the_operator(render):
+    r = render("strict")
+    env = _notif(r)
+    assert env["EMAIL_SMTP_HOST"] == r.env["EMAIL_SMTP_HOST"] and env["EMAIL_SMTP_HOST"] != "mailhog"
+    assert env["EMAIL_SMTP_TLS_MODE"] in ("starttls", "tls")
+    assert env["NOTIFICATION_FALLBACK_ONCALL_EMAIL"] == r.env["NOTIFICATION_FALLBACK_ONCALL_EMAIL"]
+
+@pytest.mark.parametrize("source", ["EMAIL_SMTP_HOST", "EMAIL_FROM_EMAIL", "NOTIFICATION_FALLBACK_ONCALL_EMAIL"])
+def test_strict_render_requires_delivery_settings(render, source):
+    with pytest.raises(ComposeRenderError, match=source):
+        render("strict", drop=[source])
+
+def test_sms_flag_is_overridable(render):
+    assert _notif(render("strict", overrides={"SMS_ENABLED": "true"}))["SMS_ENABLED"] == "true"
+
+def test_ack_links_point_at_the_api_site(render):
+    r = render("strict")
+    assert _notif(r)["NOTIFICATION_ACK_BASE_URL"] == f"https://api.{r.env['DOMAIN']}"
+
+def test_events_enabled_on_every_app_service(render):
+    r = render("strict")
+    assert all(r.services[s]["environment"]["EVENTS_ENABLED"] == "true" for s in APP_SERVICES)
+
+def test_env_example_names_what_the_code_reads():
+    keys = {l.split("=", 1)[0] for l in (REPO / ".env.example").read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines() if "=" in l and not l.startswith("#")}
+    assert {"EMAIL_SMTP_HOST", "EMAIL_SMTP_TLS_MODE", "EMAIL_FROM_EMAIL", "NOTIFICATION_FALLBACK_ONCALL_EMAIL"} <= keys
+    assert not keys & {"SMTP_HOST", "SMTP_PORT", "EMAIL_FROM", "TWILIO_ACCOUNT_SID", "FCM_PROJECT_ID"}
+
+def test_caddyfile_routes_crisis_acknowledgements_to_notification():
+    text = (REPO / "Caddyfile").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
+    block = re.search(r"handle /api/v1/notifications/crisis-alerts/\*\s*\{(.*?)\n\s*\}\s*\n", text, re.S).group(1)
+    assert "reverse_proxy notification-service:8003" in block and "health_uri /live" in block
+
+def test_prometheus_mounts_the_rules_directory(render):
+    vols = render("dev").services["prometheus"]["volumes"]
+    assert any(v["target"] == "/etc/prometheus" and v["source"].replace("\\", "/").endswith("infrastructure/prometheus") for v in vols)
+
+@pytest.mark.integration
+@pytest.mark.realinfra
+def test_adapted_ack_route_precedes_the_orchestrator(caddy_adapt):
+    text = json.dumps(caddy_adapt({"CADDY_API_SITE": "http://api.example.test", "CADDY_AUTH_SITE": "http://auth.example.test",
+                                   "DOMAIN": "example.test", "CADDY_ADMIN_EMAIL": "ops@example.test",
+                                   "BASIC_AUTH_HASH": "$2a$14$Zkx19XLiW6VYouLHR5NmfOFU0z2GTNmpkT/5qqR7hx4IjWJPDhjvG"}))
+    assert text.index('"/api/v1/notifications/crisis-alerts/*"') < text.index('"orchestrator-service:8000"')
+```

+<!-- brief extracted from WS-04-service-runtime-observability.md -->
+### Task WS-04.6: Wire service tokens, the event plane and rate limiting into the bootstrap
+**Covers:** MVP-332
+**Severity:** low
+**Wave:** 1
+**Depends on:** WS-04.5
+**Implementer model:** opus
+**Review:** security-critical
+**Decision gate:** D-03 (default assumed: HS256 service JWTs from `ServiceTokenManager` in W1-W3), D-26 (default assumed: app-level Redis limiter; auth routes fail closed on Redis failure)
+**Real infra:** yes (BOOTSMOKE in dev and strict)
+**Cross-workstream prerequisites:** WS-06 W1 phase A1 `solace_events.service_plane.start_service_event_plane` and `ServiceEventPlane.readiness()/stop()` (IF-05; `stop()` finishes the in-flight flush and releases claims); WS-07 W1 phase A1 `solace_security.rate_limit.install_rate_limiting(app, spec)` and `ServiceTokenManager`/`ServiceAuthSettings` (IF-04); WS-05's boot smoke.
+**Files:**
+- Modify: `src/solace_infrastructure/runtime/bootstrap.py` (step 4 of `start_service_runtime`; `create_service_app` calls `install_rate_limiting(app, spec)` when `spec.rate_limited`)
+- Test: `tests/bootstrap/test_mvp_332_w1_wiring.py`
+**Interfaces:**
+- Consumes: `start_service_event_plane(service_name: str, *, pool: asyncpg.Pool | None, kafka_settings: KafkaSettings | None = None, strict: bool) -> ServiceEventPlane` (IF-05); `ServiceTokenManager(service_settings=ServiceAuthSettings(service_name=...))` and `.create_service_token(service_name)` (IF-04); `install_rate_limiting(app, spec)` (IF-04).
+- Produces: `ServiceRuntime.service_tokens` (every service), `ServiceRuntime.event_plane` (when `event_plane != "none"`), readiness checks `service_tokens` and `event_plane` (critical); closer order guarantees `event_plane.stop()` runs before the Postgres pool closes.
+
+- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test** in `tests/bootstrap/test_mvp_332_w1_wiring.py`
+```python
+"""MVP-332 (event plane): the plane is started by the runtime, gated by SPEC, and stopped before the pool closes."""
+import pytest
+from fastapi import FastAPI
+from solace_infrastructure.runtime import (ServiceRuntimeSpec, StartupDependencyError,
+                                          create_service_app, start_service_runtime, stop_service_runtime)
+from solace_infrastructure.runtime import bootstrap as bs
+# `calls` and `fakes` come from tests/bootstrap/conftest.py (WS-04.4)
+
+class Plane:
+    def __init__(self, calls):
+        self.calls = calls
+    def readiness(self):
+        return True, "connected"
+    async def stop(self):
+        self.calls.append("plane.stop")
+
+def _spec(env="staging", **kw):
+    base = dict(uses_postgres=True, uses_redis=True, event_plane="publish_consume")
+    base.update(kw)
+    return ServiceRuntimeSpec(service_name="notification-service", environment=env, **base)
+
+async def test_event_plane_started_with_pool_and_strictness(fakes, calls, monkeypatch):
+    seen = {}
+    async def fake_start(service_name, *, pool, kafka_settings=None, strict):
+        seen.update(service_name=service_name, pool=pool, strict=strict)
+        return Plane(calls)
+    monkeypatch.setattr(bs, "start_service_event_plane", fake_start)
+    rt = await start_service_runtime(FastAPI(), _spec())
+    assert seen == {"service_name": "notification-service", "pool": fakes, "strict": True}
+    report = await rt.evaluate_readiness()
+    assert report.checks["event_plane"].critical and report.checks["event_plane"].ok
+    await stop_service_runtime(rt)
+    assert calls.index("plane.stop") < calls.index("pg.close_all")
+
+async def test_no_event_plane_when_spec_says_none(fakes, monkeypatch):
+    monkeypatch.setattr(bs, "start_service_event_plane", pytest.fail)
+    rt = await start_service_runtime(FastAPI(), _spec(event_plane="none"))
+    assert rt.event_plane is None
+    await stop_service_runtime(rt)
+
+async def test_strict_event_plane_failure_is_fatal(fakes, monkeypatch):
+    async def boom(*a, **k):
+        raise ConnectionError("kafka down")
+    monkeypatch.setattr(bs, "start_service_event_plane", boom)
+    with pytest.raises(StartupDependencyError) as exc:
+        await start_service_runtime(FastAPI(), _spec("production"))
+    assert exc.value.component == "event_plane"
+
+async def test_dev_event_plane_failure_degrades_ready(fakes, monkeypatch):
+    async def boom(*a, **k):
+        raise ConnectionError("kafka down")
+    monkeypatch.setattr(bs, "start_service_event_plane", boom)
+    rt = await start_service_runtime(FastAPI(), _spec("development"))
+    assert (await rt.evaluate_readiness()).checks["event_plane"].ok is False
+    await stop_service_runtime(rt)
+
+async def test_service_tokens_are_minted_at_startup(fakes, monkeypatch):
+    monkeypatch.setenv("AUTH_SECRET_KEY", "x" * 48)
+    monkeypatch.setattr(bs, "start_service_event_plane", lambda *a, **k: None)
+    rt = await start_service_runtime(FastAPI(), _spec("development", event_plane="none"))
+    assert rt.service_tokens is not None
+    assert rt.service_tokens.create_service_token("notification-service").token
+    await stop_service_runtime(rt)
+
+def test_rate_limiting_installed_only_when_flagged(monkeypatch):
+    seen = []
+    monkeypatch.setattr(bs, "install_rate_limiting", lambda app, spec: seen.append(spec.service_name))
+    for flag in (True, False):
+        spec = ServiceRuntimeSpec(service_name="user-service", environment="development", rate_limited=flag)
+        create_service_app(spec, lifespan=None, routers=[], title="t")
+    assert seen == ["user-service"]
+```
+(`ServiceCredentials.token` is the existing attribute in `src/solace_security/service_auth.py`.)
+- [ ] **Step 2: Run it and confirm it fails**
+Run: `UNIT D:\Repo\Contextual-Chatbot\tests\bootstrap\test_mvp_332_w1_wiring.py`
+Expected: FAIL with `AttributeError: module 'solace_infrastructure.runtime.bootstrap' has no attribute 'start_service_event_plane'` (and `install_rate_limiting`); the plane is not started, not checked and not stopped by any runtime, which leaves `OutboxPoller.stop` cancelling mid-flush with nothing ordering it before the pool close (MVP-332).
+- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** step 4 of `start_service_runtime` in `bootstrap.py`. Approach: module-level imports `from solace_events.service_plane import start_service_event_plane`, `from solace_security.rate_limit import install_rate_limiting`, `from solace_security.service_auth import ServiceAuthSettings, ServiceTokenManager`; the token manager is built for every service and proven by minting one token (readiness `service_tokens`); the plane gets `pool=runtime.pg_pool` and `strict=is_strict_environment(spec.environment)`, its `readiness` is wrapped as an async check, and `plane.stop` is registered as a closer after the pool closer is registered (reverse order then stops the plane first). The rate limiter is installed inside `create_service_app` between CORS and the request-context middleware in the outer-to-inner order fixed in WS-04.3.
+- [ ] **Step 4: Run it and confirm it passes**
+Run: same command. Expected: PASS

Expected: all pass; in strict no container log contains an in-memory or localhost fallback warning.

+<!-- brief extracted from WS-04-service-runtime-observability.md -->
+### Task WS-04.7: Alertmanager and the crisis-path alert rules, promtool-tested
+**Covers:** MVP-036
+**Severity:** high
+**Wave:** 1
+**Depends on:** WS-04.5, WS-03.6
+**Implementer model:** opus
+**Review:** safety-critical
+**Decision gate:** D-12 (default assumed: email through an SMTP relay with STARTTLS; mailpit in tests)
+**Real infra:** yes (promtool and amtool in pinned images; e2e delivery through the IF-19 stack)
+**Cross-workstream prerequisites:** WS-03.6 registers the `ALERTMANAGER_*` variables and mounts `./infrastructure/prometheus` at `/etc/prometheus` in the base compose; WS-06 W1 metrics `solace_outbox_oldest_pending_seconds{service}`, `solace_consumer_lag{group,topic}` (from WS-06.10 sampled by every plane for the crisis pair, crisis-review F13), `solace_dlq_unresolved{group}`, `solace_event_publish_failures_total{service}`; WS-13 W1 metrics `solace_crisis_alert_delivery_total{outcome,channel}` (outcome values requested below) and `solace_crisis_alerts_unacknowledged`; WS-05 includes this fragment in the e2e stack and points Alertmanager at mailpit (requested below).
+**Files:**
+- Create: `docker-compose.observability.yml` (service `alertmanager`: image `prom/alertmanager:v0.27.0`, entrypoint `["/bin/sh", "/etc/alertmanager-src/render-config.sh"]`, volume `./infrastructure/alertmanager:/etc/alertmanager-src:ro` and named volume `alertmanager_data:/alertmanager`, tmpfs `/run/alertmanager`, environment from the six `ALERTMANAGER_*` registry variables with `${ALERTMANAGER_EMAIL_TO:?...}` and `${ALERTMANAGER_SMTP_SMARTHOST:?...}` (no default, so the fragment can never page a placeholder address), network `monitoring`, no published port; no base service is redefined)
+- Create: `infrastructure/prometheus/rules/solace.yml`, `infrastructure/prometheus/tests/solace_rules_test.yml`, `infrastructure/alertmanager/alertmanager.yml.tmpl`, `infrastructure/alertmanager/render-config.sh`, `docs/runbooks/alerting.md`
+- Modify: `infrastructure/prometheus/prometheus.yml` (`rule_files: ["/etc/prometheus/rules/*.yml"]`; `alerting.alertmanagers` → `alertmanager:9093`)
+- Test: `tests/bootstrap/test_mvp_036_alert_rules.py`, `tests/e2e/test_mvp_036_alert_delivery.py`
+**Interfaces:**
+- Consumes: IF-05 and IF-09 metric names above; `solace_service_ready{service}` and `up{job="solace-services"}` (WS-04.3, WS-04.5).
+- Produces: alerts `CrisisAlertDeliveryFailed` (`sum by (channel) (increase(solace_crisis_alert_delivery_total{outcome="failed"}[5m])) > 0`), `CrisisOutboxBacklog` (`max by (service) (solace_outbox_oldest_pending_seconds) > 120` for 1m), `NotificationConsumerLag` (`max by (group, topic) (solace_consumer_lag{group="solace-group-notifications"}) > 10` for 5m; `max`, not `sum`, because from WS-06.10 every event plane also samples the crisis group's lag, crisis-review F13), `CrisisConsumerStalled` (`max by (group, topic) (solace_consumer_lag{group="solace-group-notifications",topic="solace.safety"}) > 0` for 3m, crisis-review F13: one stuck crisis turn is only 4 messages, so the volume rule above never sees a consumer that is alive but makes no progress), `DLQUnresolved` (`sum by (group) (solace_dlq_unresolved) > 0` for 1m), `CrisisAlertsUnacknowledged` (`max(solace_crisis_alerts_unacknowledged) > 0` for 20m), `CrisisEventPublishFailures` (`sum by (service) (increase(solace_event_publish_failures_total[5m])) > 0`), `ServiceNotReady` (`(up{job="solace-services"} == 0) or (solace_service_ready == 0)` for 2m); every rule has `labels.severity: page`, crisis-path rules `labels.path: crisis`, and `annotations.runbook_url` pointing at `docs/runbooks/alerting.md#<lower-case alert name>`; Alertmanager routes everything to `ALERTMANAGER_EMAIL_TO`, grouping by `alertname` and `service`, `group_wait 30s`, `group_interval 5m`, `repeat_interval 1h` for `path="crisis"` and `4h` otherwise, `send_resolved: true`. `docs/runbooks/alerting.md` is created and edited only by WS-04 (owned path); WS-13.10's `docs/runbooks/crisis-escalation.md` links to it read-only for the WS-04 alerts, which this task creates in W1 phase A, ahead of WS-13.10 in phase B.
+
+- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test** in `tests/bootstrap/test_mvp_036_alert_rules.py` and `tests/e2e/test_mvp_036_alert_delivery.py`
+```python
+# tests/bootstrap/test_mvp_036_alert_rules.py
+"""MVP-036 (part 2): crisis-path alert rules exist, reference real metric names, carry no user data,
+fire under promtool series fixtures, and Alertmanager routes them to the ops mailbox."""
+import re, subprocess
+from pathlib import Path
+import pytest, yaml
+
+REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
+RULES = REPO / "infrastructure/prometheus/rules/solace.yml"
+W1_ALERTS = {"CrisisAlertDeliveryFailed": "solace_crisis_alert_delivery_total", "CrisisOutboxBacklog": "solace_outbox_oldest_pending_seconds",
+             "NotificationConsumerLag": "solace_consumer_lag", "CrisisConsumerStalled": "solace_consumer_lag", "DLQUnresolved": "solace_dlq_unresolved",
+             "CrisisAlertsUnacknowledged": "solace_crisis_alerts_unacknowledged", "CrisisEventPublishFailures": "solace_event_publish_failures_total",
+             "ServiceNotReady": "solace_service_ready"}
+
+def _rules():
+    return {r["alert"]: r for g in yaml.safe_load(RULES.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))["groups"] for r in g["rules"]}
+
+def test_every_w1_alert_exists_and_uses_its_metric():
+    rules = _rules()
+    for alert, metric in W1_ALERTS.items():
+        assert metric in rules[alert]["expr"], alert
+        assert rules[alert]["labels"]["severity"] == "page"
+    assert 'up{job="solace-services"} == 0' in rules["ServiceNotReady"]["expr"]
+
+def test_crisis_review_f13_lag_rules_use_max_and_the_stall_rule_fires_on_any_lag():
+    rules = _rules()
+    for alert in ("NotificationConsumerLag", "CrisisConsumerStalled"):
+        assert rules[alert]["expr"].lstrip().startswith("max by (group, topic)"), alert   # several planes export the pair
+    stalled = rules["CrisisConsumerStalled"]
+    assert 'topic="solace.safety"' in stalled["expr"] and stalled["expr"].rstrip().endswith("> 0")
+    assert stalled["for"] == "3m" and stalled["labels"]["path"] == "crisis"
+
+def test_annotations_never_template_user_data():
+    for alert, rule in _rules().items():
+        text = yaml.safe_dump(rule.get("annotations", {}))
+        assert not re.search(r"\$labels\.(user_id|email|message|session_id)", text), alert
+
+def test_every_alert_has_a_runbook_section():
+    doc = (REPO / "docs/runbooks/alerting.md").read_text(encoding="utf-8").lower()
+    for alert, rule in _rules().items():
+        anchor = rule["annotations"]["runbook_url"].split("#")[1]
+        assert anchor == alert.lower() and f"## {alert.lower()}" in doc, alert
+
+def test_prometheus_loads_rules_and_alertmanager():
+    cfg = yaml.safe_load((REPO / "infrastructure/prometheus/prometheus.yml").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
+    assert "/etc/prometheus/rules/*.yml" in cfg["rule_files"]
+    assert cfg["alerting"]["alertmanagers"][0]["static_configs"][0]["targets"] == ["alertmanager:9093"]
+
+def test_fragment_adds_alertmanager_without_redefining_base_services():
+    frag = yaml.safe_load((REPO / "docker-compose.observability.yml").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
+    base = yaml.safe_load((REPO / "docker-compose.yml").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
+    am = frag["services"]["alertmanager"]
+    assert am["image"] == "prom/alertmanager:v0.27.0" and "ports" not in am
+    for name, svc in frag["services"].items():
+        if name in base["services"]:
+            assert "image" not in svc and "build" not in svc, name
+
+@pytest.mark.integration
+@pytest.mark.realinfra
+def test_promtool_rule_unit_tests_pass():
+    out = subprocess.run(["docker", "run", "--rm", "-v", f"{REPO / 'infrastructure' / 'prometheus'}:/etc/prometheus:ro",
+                          "--entrypoint", "promtool", "prom/prometheus:v2.47.0", "test", "rules", "/etc/prometheus/tests/solace_rules_test.yml"],
+                         capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=180)
+    assert out.returncode == 0, out.stdout + out.stderr
+
+@pytest.mark.integration
+@pytest.mark.realinfra
+def test_rendered_alertmanager_config_is_valid(tmp_path):
+    out = subprocess.run(["docker", "run", "--rm", "-v", f"{REPO / 'infrastructure' / 'alertmanager'}:/etc/alertmanager-src:ro",
+                          "-e", "ALERTMANAGER_EMAIL_TO=ops@example.test", "-e", "ALERTMANAGER_EMAIL_FROM=alerts@example.test",
+                          "-e", "ALERTMANAGER_SMTP_SMARTHOST=smtp.example.test:587", "-e", "ALERTMANAGER_SMTP_REQUIRE_TLS=true",
+                          "--entrypoint", "/bin/sh", "prom/alertmanager:v0.27.0", "-c",
+                          "RENDER_ONLY=1 /bin/sh /etc/alertmanager-src/render-config.sh && amtool check-config /run/alertmanager/alertmanager.yml"],
+                         capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=180)
+    assert out.returncode == 0, out.stdout + out.stderr
+
+@pytest.mark.integration
+@pytest.mark.realinfra
+def test_render_refuses_missing_recipient():
+    out = subprocess.run(["docker", "run", "--rm", "-v", f"{REPO / 'infrastructure' / 'alertmanager'}:/etc/alertmanager-src:ro",
+                          "--entrypoint", "/bin/sh", "prom/alertmanager:v0.27.0", "-c", "RENDER_ONLY=1 /bin/sh /etc/alertmanager-src/render-config.sh"],
+                         capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=120)
+    assert out.returncode == 64 and "ALERTMANAGER_EMAIL_TO" in out.stderr
+```
+```python
+# tests/e2e/test_mvp_036_alert_delivery.py
+"""MVP-036: stopping notification-service pages the ops mailbox via Prometheus -> Alertmanager -> SMTP, with no PHI."""
+import re, time
+import httpx
+import pytest
+
+pytestmark = [pytest.mark.e2e, pytest.mark.integration]
+
+def test_service_not_ready_reaches_mailpit(e2e_stack, fault):
+    fault.stop("notification-service")
+    try:
+        deadline = time.monotonic() + 420
+        hit = None
+        while time.monotonic() < deadline and hit is None:
+            msgs = httpx.get(f"{e2e_stack.mailpit_api}/api/v1/messages", timeout=10).json()["messages"]
+            hit = next((m for m in msgs if "ServiceNotReady" in m["Subject"]), None)
+            time.sleep(10)
+        assert hit is not None, "ServiceNotReady alert email not delivered within 7 minutes"
+        body = httpx.get(f"{e2e_stack.mailpit_api}/api/v1/message/{hit['ID']}", timeout=10).json()["Text"]
+        assert "notification-service" in body
+        emails = set(re.findall(r"[A-Za-z0-9._%+-]+@[A-Za-z0-9.-]+\.[A-Za-z]{2,}", body))
+        assert emails <= {"ops-alerts@test.invalid", "alerts@test.invalid"}
+    finally:
+        fault.start("notification-service")
+```
+(`fault` is the IF-19 fault helper; if WS-05 exposes it as an attribute of `e2e_stack` instead of a fixture, the implementer uses that form.) `solace_rules_test.yml` contains, for each W1 alert, one firing case with input series at the threshold plus margin and the alert's `for` duration elapsed, and one non-firing case (`outcome="delivered"` increments, lag 10, oldest pending 119 s, `solace_service_ready` 1 with `up` 1). For `CrisisConsumerStalled` (crisis-review F13) the firing case is lag 4 held for 3 minutes, exported by two series that differ only in `service` (two planes sampling the same pair), and the non-firing case is lag 4 for one evaluation, then 0; the `NotificationConsumerLag` non-firing case also has lag 6 from each of two such series (which `sum` would push over 10). `render-config.sh` exits 64 with the missing variable's name on stderr when `ALERTMANAGER_EMAIL_TO` or `ALERTMANAGER_SMTP_SMARTHOST` is unset, writes `/run/alertmanager/alertmanager.yml` with `sed` substitutions, omits the auth lines when `ALERTMANAGER_SMTP_USERNAME` is empty, writes the SMTP password to `/run/alertmanager/smtp_password` and references it with `smtp_auth_password_file`, and execs `/bin/alertmanager --config.file=/run/alertmanager/alertmanager.yml --storage.path=/alertmanager` unless `RENDER_ONLY=1`.

The e2e test (`$env:SOLACE_E2E_PROFILE="strict"; $env:SOLACE_E2E_ENVIRONMENT="production"; UNIT D:\Repo\Contextual-Chatbot\tests\e2e\test_mvp_036_alert_delivery.py`) fails with "ServiceNotReady alert email not delivered within 7 minutes".

Approach: rule expressions exactly as listed under Produces; the email template uses Alertmanager's default text body plus a subject `[Solace ALERT {{ .Status | toUpper }}] {{ .CommonLabels.alertname }} {{ .CommonLabels.service }}`; labels only ever hold service, group, topic, channel and store names; the runbook has one `## <alert name>` section per alert with meaning, first checks and escalation.

+- Test: `tests/integration/test_sprint7_portkey_fallback.py`
+
+**Interfaces:**
+- Consumes: IF-15 `LLMUnavailableError` with `reason == "timeout"` (WS-09.3); `UnifiedLLMClient(settings, client_factory=...)` (WS-09.2).
+- Produces:
+  - Satisfies WS-09's request CR-6 for WS-09.3. The node `TestLLMRequestTimeout::test_generate_returns_empty_on_timeout_not_hang`, which WS-09.3's Step 5 records as failing, is retired in the same wave.
+  - Steward sanction for WS-16's request R12 (WS-16.5, WS-16.11, both W2). WS-16.11 rewrites `tests/integration/test_diagnosis_vignette.py`, and WS-16.5 edits only class `TestDiagnosisBridgeSafetyFlags` in `tests/integration/test_event_flow.py`. Both edits are made by the owner of the code under test, per the `tests/integration` shared-file protocol. WS-05 makes no edit to either file. The full register of sanctioned edits is under "Shared-directory stewardship" below.
+
+- [ ] **Step 1: Write the replacement test** in `tests/integration/test_sprint7_portkey_fallback.py` (class `TestLLMRequestTimeout`):
+```python
+    @pytest.mark.asyncio
+    async def test_generate_raises_unavailable_on_timeout(self) -> None:
+        """REV-31 + IF-15: a stalled provider surfaces as LLMUnavailableError(reason="timeout") within the bound, never '' or a hang."""
+        from services.shared.infrastructure.llm_client import LLMUnavailableError
+
+        async def _hang(**kwargs):  # noqa: ANN003
+            await asyncio.sleep(30)
+
+        fake = types.SimpleNamespace(chat=types.SimpleNamespace(completions=types.SimpleNamespace(create=_hang)))
+        settings = LLMClientSettings(_env_file=None, portkey_api_key=SecretStr("test-portkey-key"), timeout_seconds=1)
+        client = UnifiedLLMClient(settings, client_factory=lambda **_: fake)
+        await client.initialize()
+        loop = asyncio.get_running_loop(); t0 = loop.time()
+        with pytest.raises(LLMUnavailableError) as exc:
+            await client.generate(system_prompt="s", user_message="hi", service_name="test")
+        assert exc.value.reason == "timeout" and loop.time() - t0 < 5.0
+```
+- [ ] **Step 2: Confirm the red.** Before replacing the old test, run: `$env:PYTHONPATH="D:\Repo\Contextual-Chatbot;D:\Repo\Contextual-Chatbot\src"; $env:ENVIRONMENT="test"; & "D:\tmp\mvp-exec\venv\Scripts\python.exe" -m pytest "tests/integration/test_sprint7_portkey_fallback.py::TestLLMRequestTimeout::test_generate_returns_empty_on_timeout_not_hang" -o "addopts=" -p no:logging -q`. Expected: FAIL with `LLMUnavailableError`, because WS-09.3 made `generate()` raise instead of returning `""`. The old assertion `result == ""` now pins the retired sentinel behaviour (MVP-139). On the pre-WS-09.3 tree, the new test fails with `ImportError: cannot import name 'LLMUnavailableError'`.
+- [ ] **Step 3: Implement.** Delete `test_generate_returns_empty_on_timeout_not_hang` and add the Step 1 test in its place. This is a test-only change.
+- [ ] **Step 4: Run it and confirm it passes.** Run: the unit command with `"tests/integration/test_sprint7_portkey_fallback.py::TestLLMRequestTimeout"`. Expected: PASS (2 tests).
+- [ ] **Step 5: Regression.** Run the unit command with `tests/integration`. Expected: all pass. WS-09.3's remaining recorded failures are therapy-service nodes (its CR-5), outside this directory.
+- [ ] **Step 6: Checkpoint:** `& D:\tmp\mvp-exec\tools\checkpoint.ps1 -Label "WS-05.20:done"` and record the printed tree id in the ledger. No git commit.

+### Task WS-05.21: W1 overlay env sync: the D-04 Bedrock wiring to the fake LLM and test-traffic auth rate limits
+**Covers:** MVP-039
+**Severity:** high
+**Wave:** 1
+**Depends on:** WS-05.4
+**Implementer model:** sonnet
+**Review:** security-critical
+**Decision gate:** D-04 (decided 2026-10-19: real-user LLM traffic goes to Anthropic models on Bedrock, and tests use only the fake. This task follows WS-09's CR-3 as revised for D-04. The dev profile reaches WS-05.1's Converse route with `LLM_PROVIDER=bedrock`, `BEDROCK_ENDPOINT_URL=http://fake-llm:8080`, the synthetic `BEDROCK_MODEL_ID=fake-llm-model` (never a real AWS model id) and `LLM_PHI_EGRESS_APPROVED=true`; it carries no AWS credential, because WS-09.18 sends unsigned requests when the override is set. Strict refuses the override (WS-09.18), so the strict profile sets `LLM_PROVIDER=bedrock` and `LLM_PHI_EGRESS_APPROVED=false` and runs template replies, exactly as a strict deployment does until the AWS BAA is recorded. Neither profile sets `LLM_PORTKEY_*` or a provider API key). D-26 (default assumed: the app-level limiter is the control; the overrides are a test-overlay value only).
+**Real infra:** no (it renders `docker compose config`; no container starts)
+**Files:**
+- Modify: `docker-compose.test.yml`.
+  - On orchestrator, safety, therapy, diagnosis and personality: `LLM_PROVIDER=bedrock` and `LLM_PHI_EGRESS_APPROVED=false` replace WS-05.4's `LLM_PORTKEY_GATEWAY_URL`, `LLM_PHI_EGRESS_APPROVED=true` and `LLM_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=""`. `LLM_ENABLE_CACHING=false` stays.
+  - On every app service: `RATE_LIMIT_OVERRIDES_JSON: '{"auth-login": 500, "auth-register": 500, "auth-refresh": 500, "auth-verify": 500}'`.
+- Create: `infrastructure/test/compose/llm-dev.yml`, the dev-only fragment. On the same five services it sets `BEDROCK_ENDPOINT_URL=http://fake-llm:8080`, `BEDROCK_MODEL_ID=fake-llm-model` and `LLM_PHI_EGRESS_APPROVED=true`. It is never part of a strict render, so no strict stack carries the override.
+- Modify: `src/solace_testing/e2e.py`.
+  - `StackConfig.compose_files` appends `infrastructure/test/compose/llm-dev.yml` after `docker-compose.test.yml` in the dev profile only.
+  - `OVERLAY_ALLOWED_KEYS` gains `LLM_PROVIDER` and `RATE_LIMIT_OVERRIDES_JSON` and loses `LLM_PORTKEY_GATEWAY_URL` and `LLM_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`.
+- Create: `tests/solace_testing/test_mvp_039_overlay_w1_env.py`
+- Test: `tests/solace_testing/test_mvp_039_overlay_w1_env.py`
+
+**Interfaces:**
+- Consumes:
+  - IF-15 as amended by D-04: `LLM_PROVIDER`, `BEDROCK_ENDPOINT_URL`, `BEDROCK_MODEL_ID` and `LLM_PHI_EGRESS_APPROVED`, with WS-09.18's unsigned override and its strict refusal, and WS-09.4's approval rule;
+  - the registry entries WS-09 requested from WS-03 (CR-14, CR-4), so `tests/deploy/test_env_contract.py` accepts the overlay;
+  - WS-05.1's Converse route;
+  - IF-04 `RATE_LIMIT_OVERRIDES_JSON` and the route names `auth-login`, `auth-register`, `auth-refresh`, `auth-verify` (WS-07.7).
+- Produces:
+  - Satisfies WS-09's CR-3 for WS-09.4 and WS-09.18: strict services boot with the LLM off, and dev services reach the fake as Bedrock.
+  - Satisfies WS-07's request for WS-07.7: `make_user` loops never share the 10/min register and 20/min login buckets that all test traffic hits behind Caddy.
+  - No profile carries an LLM key, so WS-05.13 (W3) has none to move to `_FILE`.
+
+- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests.** In `tests/solace_testing/test_mvp_039_overlay_w1_env.py`:
+```python
+LLM_SERVICES = ("orchestrator-service", "safety-service", "therapy-service", "diagnosis-service", "personality-service")
+NO_LLM_KEYS = ("LLM_PORTKEY_API_KEY", "LLM_PORTKEY_GATEWAY_URL", "LLM_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY",
+               "AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID", "AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY", "AWS_SESSION_TOKEN", "AWS_PROFILE")
+
+def _render(tmp_path, profile, monkeypatch):
+    monkeypatch.setenv("SOLACE_E2E_PROFILE", profile)
+    run = tmp_path / profile
+    subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "scripts.test.gen_e2e_env", "--profile", profile, "--environment",
+                    "development" if profile == "dev" else "production", "--out", str(run)], cwd=REPO, check=True, capture_output=True)
+    args = [a for f in StackConfig.from_env().compose_files for a in ("-f", f)]     # exactly the files the fixture uses
+    out = subprocess.run(["docker", "compose", "--env-file", str(run / "e2e.env"), *args, "config", "--format", "json"],
+                         cwd=REPO, capture_output=True, text=True, check=True).stdout      # never printed: it holds secrets
+    return json.loads(out)["services"]
+
+def test_d04_dev_stacks_reach_the_fake_llm_as_bedrock(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
+    services = _render(tmp_path, "dev", monkeypatch)
+    for svc in LLM_SERVICES:
+        env = services[svc]["environment"]
+        assert (env["LLM_PROVIDER"], env["BEDROCK_ENDPOINT_URL"], env["BEDROCK_MODEL_ID"], env["LLM_PHI_EGRESS_APPROVED"]) == (
+            "bedrock", "http://fake-llm:8080", "fake-llm-model", "true"), svc
+        assert not any(env.get(k) for k in NO_LLM_KEYS), svc
+
+def test_d04_strict_stacks_run_bedrock_with_the_llm_off_and_no_override(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
+    services = _render(tmp_path, "strict", monkeypatch)
+    for svc in LLM_SERVICES:
+        env = services[svc]["environment"]
+        assert env["LLM_PROVIDER"] == "bedrock" and env["LLM_PHI_EGRESS_APPROVED"] == "false", svc
+        assert not env.get("BEDROCK_ENDPOINT_URL") and not any(env.get(k) for k in NO_LLM_KEYS), svc
+
+@pytest.mark.parametrize("profile", ["dev", "strict"])
+def test_mvp_039_auth_buckets_are_raised_for_test_traffic(tmp_path, profile, monkeypatch):
+    for svc, spec in _render(tmp_path, profile, monkeypatch).items():
+        if svc in MVP_SERVICES:
+            overrides = json.loads(spec["environment"]["RATE_LIMIT_OVERRIDES_JSON"])
+            assert all(overrides[r] >= 500 for r in ("auth-login", "auth-register", "auth-refresh", "auth-verify"))
+```
+- [ ] **Step 2: Run them and confirm they fail.** Run: unit command with `tests/solace_testing/test_mvp_039_overlay_w1_env.py`. Expected: FAIL.
+  - Both D-04 tests raise `KeyError: 'LLM_PROVIDER'`, because WS-05.4's overlay wires only the Portkey gateway. The dev test also lacks `BEDROCK_ENDPOINT_URL`, since no dev-only fragment exists.
+  - `RATE_LIMIT_OVERRIDES_JSON` is missing (`KeyError`).
+  - Once WS-09.18 lands, a strict service without `LLM_PROVIDER=bedrock` fails startup by design (the default is `portkey`). Once WS-07.7 is wired, the eleventh `make_user` in a minute gets 429. Either way every W1 acceptance suite would go red for a harness reason (MVP-039).
+- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** the overlay entries, the dev-only fragment, `compose_files` and the allowed keys. The fragment holds no secret and no credential.
+- [ ] **Step 4: Run them and confirm they pass.** Run the same command. Expected: PASS.
+- [ ] **Step 5: Regression.** Run one process each:
+  - unit command with `tests/scripts`;
+  - unit command with `tests/solace_testing`;
+  - E2E command (strict, production) with `tests/e2e/test_w0_boot_smoke.py` and `tests/e2e/test_mvp_039_stack_selftest.py`;
+  - E2E command (dev) with `tests/e2e/test_mvp_039_stack_selftest.py`.
+
+  Expected: all pass, the overlay-allowed-keys self-test included.
+- [ ] **Step 6: Checkpoint:** `& D:\tmp\mvp-exec\tools\checkpoint.ps1 -Label "WS-05.21:done"` and record the printed tree id in the ledger. No git commit.
+
+
+
+## Controller addendum (W1-prep, R-5)

+### Task WS-05.22: Deployed-configuration phrase-set recall E2E written red
+**Covers:** MVP-039
+**Severity:** high
+**Wave:** 1
+**Depends on:** WS-05.8, WS-05.21, WS-12.3
+**Implementer model:** opus
+**Review:** safety-critical
+**Decision gate:** D-05 (default assumed: provisional lexicon v1 and its 200+ phrase set; the final phrase set and floors are gated on clinical sign-off). The test never lowers a floor: each positive must reach at least its `min_level`.
+**Real infra:** yes
+**Files:**
+- Create: `tests/e2e/phrase_recall/conftest.py`. It overrides `e2e_stack` with a session fixture on a separate compose project: `cfg = StackConfig.from_env()`, then `start_stack(dataclasses.replace(cfg, lane=cfg.lane + "p", project=f"solace-e2e-{cfg.lane}p", run_dir=cfg.run_dir.with_name(f"solace-e2e-{cfg.lane}p")))`, with `stop_stack` at teardown. Its 200+ crisis episodes, re-pages included, therefore never reach the shared roster of the main lane's suites. This keeps within the two-concurrent-stacks limit.
+- Create: `tests/e2e/phrase_recall/test_w1_phrase_set_deployed.py`
+- Modify: `tests/e2e/expected_failures.yaml` (entry `target_wave: 1`, both profiles)
+- Test: `tests/e2e/phrase_recall/test_w1_phrase_set_deployed.py`
+
+**Interfaces:**
+- Consumes: IF-07 `solace_clinical.phrases.load_phrase_set() -> PhraseSet` (`positive: tuple[PositivePhrase(text, min_level, categories), ...]`) and `level_rank` (WS-12.2, WS-12.3); IF-12 REST `crisis` object; IF-19 `make_user` and `Stack.edge`; the manifest `hop()`.
+- Produces: the end-to-end recall check for review focus 1. Every clinician-reviewed positive, including the misspelled, slang, leetspeak, code-switched, emoji, quoted and earlier-clause-negation variants that WS-12 keeps in the phrase set, is screened by the deployed images and configuration, not only by in-process unit tests. A lexicon file missing from an image, or an env value that changes a floor, fails here.
+
+- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test** in `tests/e2e/phrase_recall/test_w1_phrase_set_deployed.py`:
+```python
+pytestmark = [pytest.mark.e2e, pytest.mark.integration, pytest.mark.timeout(2400)]
+
+async def test_every_phrase_set_positive_is_detected_by_the_deployed_stack(e2e_stack):
+    with hop("phrase_set"):
+        positives = load_phrase_set().positive
+        assert len(positives) >= 200
+    sem, misses = asyncio.Semaphore(4), []
+
+    async def one(i, phrase):
+        async with sem:
+            user = await make_user(e2e_stack)                 # a fresh user per phrase: no risk history carries over
+            r = await e2e_stack.edge("POST", "/api/v1/orchestrator/chat", token=user.access_token, json={"message": phrase.text})
+            crisis = r.json().get("crisis") if r.status_code == 200 else None
+            if crisis is None or level_rank(CrisisLevel(crisis["level"])) < level_rank(phrase.min_level):
+                misses.append((i, phrase.min_level.value, crisis["level"] if crisis else None))   # index only, never the text
+
+    with hop("deployed_phrase_recall"):
+        await asyncio.gather(*(one(i, p) for i, p in enumerate(positives)))
+        assert misses == [], f"{len(misses)} positives below their floor on the deployed stack: {sorted(misses)[:20]}"
+```
+- [ ] **Step 2: Run it and confirm it fails.** Run: E2E command (dev) with `tests/e2e/phrase_recall` and `SOLACE_MANIFEST_IGNORE=1`. Expected: FAIL at hop `deployed_phrase_recall` with every positive listed as a miss. The response carries no IF-12 `crisis` object before W1 phase B, so the deployed stack has never been checked against the signed-off phrase set (MVP-039).
+- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** the conftest and the test. Add the manifest entry (hop `deployed_phrase_recall`, `target_wave: 1`, both profiles, reason naming WS-10/WS-11 for the crisis object and WS-12 for recall).
+- [ ] **Step 4: Run it and confirm it passes.** Run the same command without `SOLACE_MANIFEST_IGNORE`. Expected: exit 0 with the test XFAIL at `deployed_phrase_recall`. At the W1 barrier it must pass in both profiles. A miss is a clinical-safety finding for WS-12 and is never fixed by editing the phrase set from this lane.
+- [ ] **Step 5: Regression.** Run the E2E command (dev) with `tests/e2e`. Expected: pass or XFAIL as listed. The `phrase_recall` directory builds its own stack.
+- [ ] **Step 6: Checkpoint:** `& D:\tmp\mvp-exec\tools\checkpoint.ps1 -Label "WS-05.22:done"` and record the printed tree id in the ledger. No git commit.
+

+### Task WS-05.7: Contract-test framework and crisis-chain event contracts (IF-20)
+**Covers:** MVP-357
+**Severity:** medium
+**Wave:** 1
+**Depends on:** WS-12.2
+**Implementer model:** opus
+**Review:** safety-critical
+**Decision gate:** D-05 (default assumed: `solace_clinical.CATEGORIES` from lexicon v1 are the allowed trigger codes)
+**Real infra:** no
+**Files:**
+- Modify: `src/solace_testing/contracts.py` (append the IF-20 API; the existing classes stay)
+- Modify: `src/solace_testing/__init__.py` (export the new names)
+- Modify: `tests/alignment/test_api_contracts.py`. Replace the three source-inspection tests of clients that W1 rewrites (IF-06/IF-11) with:
+  - `test_peer_service_urls_match_service_ports` (registry `SERVICE_*_SERVICE_URL` dev defaults against `SERVICE_PORTS`);
+  - `test_orchestrator_agents_use_versioned_provider_prefixes` (AST: every `"/api/..."` constant in the orchestrator's agent modules starts with `/api/v1/<provider>/`). The test only parses those WS-10/WS-11 modules and never edits them. The same check for the orchestrator's client module belongs to that module's owner: request 15, planned in WS-11.2;
+  - `test_notification_reaches_user_service_on_8001` (registry `SERVICE_USER_SERVICE_URL` lists notification-service and ends in `:8001`).
+- Create: `tests/contracts/README.md` (protocol below), `tests/contracts/contract.schema.json`
+- Create: the event contracts:
+  - `tests/contracts/events/orchestrator__notification/safety.crisis.detected.json`
+  - `tests/contracts/events/safety__notification/safety.crisis.detected.json`
+  - `tests/contracts/events/safety__notification/safety.escalation.triggered.json`
+  - `tests/contracts/events/safety__notification/safety.assessment.completed.json`
+  - `tests/contracts/events/therapy__notification/safety.crisis.detected.json`
+  - `tests/contracts/events/diagnosis__notification/safety.crisis.detected.json`
+- Create: `tests/contracts/test_event_contract_files.py`
+- Test: `tests/solace_testing/test_mvp_357_contract_framework.py` (new)
+
+**Interfaces:**
+- Consumes:
+  - IF-06 CrisisDetectedEvent rules: category codes, `escalation_action='page_on_call'`, `requires_human_review=True`, `metadata.correlation_id` = the turn id, `metadata.source_service` = the producer;
+  - `solace_events.schemas.EVENT_REGISTRY`;
+  - IF-07 `solace_clinical.CATEGORIES` and `parse_escalation_reason`, created by WS-12.2 earlier in W1 phase A1; this task depends on it.
+- Produces (IF-20):
+```python
+class ContractFile(BaseModel):                 # tests/contracts/<consumer>__<provider>/<interaction>.json
+    consumer: str; provider: str; interaction: str
+    request: ContractFileRequest(method: str, path: str, headers_required: list[str], body_example: dict | None)
+    response: ContractFileResponse(status: int, body_schema: dict)   # JSON Schema (draft 2020-12)
+class EventContract(BaseModel):                # tests/contracts/events/<producer>__<consumer>/<event_type>.json
+    consumer: str; producer: str; event_type: str; example: dict; rules: dict[str, str] = {}
+    # rules: "categories" | "uuid" | "equals:<json literal>" | "escalation_reason" keyed by dotted path ("metadata.source_service");
+    # "escalation_reason" (crisis-review F10): the value is "unspecified" or solace_clinical.parse_escalation_reason accepts it
+    # (the IF-06 form "auto|manual:<LEVEL>[:codes]", every code in CATEGORIES)
+def load_contracts(contracts_dir: Path) -> list[ContractFile]
+def load_event_contracts(contracts_dir: Path) -> list[EventContract]
+def verify_provider(app: FastAPI, contracts_dir: Path, *, auth_headers: Callable[[str], dict]) -> list[VerificationResult]
+    # synchronous: runs httpx.AsyncClient(transport=httpx.ASGITransport(app)) in a private event loop on a worker thread,
+    # so it works from sync tests and from inside a running loop (async tests). Raises AssertionError listing every
+    # mismatch: status, JSON-Schema errors (path + validator, never values)
+def assert_request_matches(contract: "ContractFile | Mapping[str, Any]", request: "httpx.Request | Mapping[str, Any]") -> None
+    # a raw dict (json.loads of the file) is validated into ContractFile first; checks method; path template
+    # ({name} placeholders); headers_required present; every body_example key present with the same JSON type
+    # (a null example value accepts any type); extra request keys are allowed
+def validate_event_contract(contract: EventContract) -> list[str]           # example validates via EVENT_REGISTRY; rules hold
+def verify_event_producer(contract: EventContract, produced: "BaseEvent | Mapping[str, Any]") -> list[str]
+    # same event_type; validates; rules hold; top-level key set equals the example's (catches renames); messages never echo values
+async def verify_event_consumer(contract: EventContract, handler: Callable[["BaseEvent"], Awaitable[None]]) -> None
+```
+  Protocol, in `tests/contracts/README.md`:
+  - The consumer workstream writes `tests/contracts/<consumer>__<provider>/` (WS-10 writes `orchestrator__safety`, WS-11 the other `orchestrator__*`, WS-13 `notification__user`).
+  - WS-05 writes `tests/contracts/events/**`, `client__orchestrator/` and, at WS-15's request, `therapy__memory/`.
+  - Providers call `verify_provider` in their own suites, and producers call `verify_event_producer`.
+
+- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test** in `tests/solace_testing/test_mvp_357_contract_framework.py`:
+```python
+def _provider(field="level", value="HIGH", status=200):
+    app = FastAPI()
+    @app.post("/api/v1/safety/check")
+    async def check(body: dict):
+        return JSONResponse({field: value, "risk_score": 0.9}, status_code=status)
+    return app
+
+def _contract_dir(tmp_path):
+    d = tmp_path / "orchestrator__safety"; d.mkdir()
+    (d / "check.json").write_text(json.dumps({
+        "consumer": "orchestrator", "provider": "safety", "interaction": "check",
+        "request": {"method": "POST", "path": "/api/v1/safety/check", "headers_required": ["Authorization", "X-Turn-ID"],
+                    "body_example": {"user_id": "u", "session_id": "s", "content": "c", "turn_id": "t"}},
+        "response": {"status": 200, "body_schema": {"type": "object", "required": ["level", "risk_score"],
+                     "properties": {"level": {"enum": ["NONE", "LOW", "ELEVATED", "HIGH", "CRITICAL"]}, "risk_score": {"type": "number"}}}}}))
+    return d
+
+def _auth(service): return {"Authorization": "Bearer test", "X-Turn-ID": str(uuid4())}
+
+def test_mvp_357_matching_provider_passes(tmp_path):
+    verify_provider(_provider(), _contract_dir(tmp_path), auth_headers=_auth)
+
+@pytest.mark.parametrize("kwargs,needle", [

+def test_mvp_357_provider_drift_is_reported(tmp_path, kwargs, needle):

+def test_mvp_357_consumer_request_without_turn_header_is_rejected(tmp_path):

+                                          "json": {"user_id": "u", "session_id": "s", "content": "c", "turn_id": "t"}})
+
+def test_mvp_357_raw_contract_dict_and_httpx_request_are_accepted(tmp_path):
+    raw = json.loads((_contract_dir(tmp_path) / "check.json").read_text())       # consumers pass json.loads(...) dicts
+    req = httpx.Request("POST", "http://safety/api/v1/safety/check", headers=_auth("orchestrator"),
+                        json={"user_id": "u", "session_id": "s", "content": "c", "turn_id": "t", "extra": 1})
+    assert_request_matches(raw, req)                                             # extra keys allowed
+    with pytest.raises(AssertionError, match="turn_id"):
+        assert_request_matches(raw, httpx.Request("POST", "http://safety/api/v1/safety/check", headers=_auth("o"),
+                                                  json={"user_id": "u", "session_id": "s", "content": "c"}))
+
+async def test_mvp_357_verify_provider_works_inside_a_running_event_loop(tmp_path):
+    verify_provider(_provider(), _contract_dir(tmp_path), auth_headers=_auth)    # provider suites are often async
+
+def test_mvp_357_raw_trigger_text_and_renamed_fields_are_reported():
+    contract = load_event_contracts(CONTRACTS / "events" / "diagnosis__notification")[0]
+    bad = {**contract.example, "trigger_indicators": ["I want to end my life"]}
+    errors = verify_event_producer(contract, bad)
+    assert any(e.startswith("trigger_indicators[0]") for e in errors) and not any("end my life" in e for e in errors)
+    renamed = {k: v for k, v in contract.example.items() if k != "crisis_level"} | {"level": "HIGH"}
+    assert any("crisis_level" in e for e in verify_event_producer(contract, renamed))
+
+def test_crisis_review_f10_escalation_reason_rule_accepts_the_producer_format_only():
+    contract = next(c for c in load_event_contracts(CONTRACTS / "events" / "safety__notification")
+                    if c.event_type == "safety.escalation.triggered")
+    assert contract.rules["escalation_reason"] == "escalation_reason"
+    for ok in ("auto:CRITICAL:suicidal_ideation", "auto:HIGH", "unspecified"):
+        assert verify_event_producer(contract, {**contract.example, "escalation_reason": ok}) == [], ok
+    errors = verify_event_producer(contract, {**contract.example, "escalation_reason": "Triggers: kill myself"})
+    assert any(e.startswith("escalation_reason") for e in errors) and not any("kill myself" in e for e in errors)

+<!-- brief extracted from WS-05-realinfra-harness-e2e.md -->
+### Task WS-05.8: W1 crisis fault suite and negative-phrase E2E written red
+**Covers:** MVP-039
+**Severity:** high
+**Wave:** 1
+**Depends on:** WS-05.6
+**Implementer model:** opus
+**Review:** safety-critical
+**Decision gate:** D-05 (provisional lexicon; the e2e phrases must appear in the clinical phrase set). D-06 (default assumed: 15-minute coalescing; re-page of the backup and the fallback mailbox; a same-turn rise pages again, crisis-review F8). D-22 (default assumed: the local screen stands when the safety service is down or hung, inside the 3 s budget). D-28 (notification is the only pager; a page survives a database outage, crisis-review F2).
+**Real infra:** yes
+**Files:**
+- Create: `tests/e2e/test_w1_crisis_faults.py`
+- Create: `tests/e2e/test_w1_negative_phrases.py`
+- Modify: `docker-compose.test.yml` (app services: `NOTIFICATION_REPAGE_AFTER_SECONDS=30`, `NOTIFICATION_ALERT_COALESCE_SECONDS=900`, `NOTIFICATION_MAX_REPAGES=3`)
+- Modify: `src/solace_testing/e2e.py` (`pages_since(stack, address, t0) -> list[MailMessage]`; `wait_ready(stack, service, timeout) -> HttpResult`; `crisis_episodes`, `pages_for_episode` and `wait_for_episode_page` come from WS-05.6)
+- Modify: `tests/e2e/expected_failures.yaml` (entries `target_wave: 1`, both profiles)
+- Test: the two new files
+
+**Interfaces:**
+- Consumes: IF-06, IF-09 (recipients care team, then on-call roster, then fallback; one coalesced episode; re-page), IF-10 degraded reasons, IF-12, IF-15 fake-LLM modes, IF-08 `--assign`, IF-19 `fault.pause`/`unpause`, WS-05.6's episode-scoped mail helpers, and IF-07 `tests/clinical/crisis_phrases_v1.yaml` (`positive[].text`, `negative[].text`).
+- Produces: the W1 exit-criterion fault suite, in both profiles.
+
+- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests.** In `tests/e2e/test_w1_crisis_faults.py` (`pytest.mark.timeout(1500)`). Each test provisions its own clinicians under `hop("provision_clinician")`, sends through the edge, and restores faults in `finally`. Every page count below is scoped to the test user's episode (`crisis_episodes(stack, user.id)` and `pages_for_episode`), never to a whole mailbox: the on-call clinicians that earlier tests provision stay on call, and the fallback mailbox is shared by the whole session (crisis-review F6). "1 page" means one page per distinct level of the episode, normally one (a same-turn rise to CRITICAL pages again, crisis-review F8):
+```python
+async def test_all_agents_failing_still_returns_resources_and_pages(e2e_stack):
+    # llm.scenario("error"); fault.stop("therapy-service","diagnosis-service","personality-service","memory-service")
+    # hop rest_crisis_object: crisis set, degraded non-empty; hop clinician_page: exactly 1 page to the care-team clinician in 60 s
+async def test_notification_down_two_minutes_pages_once_after_restart(e2e_stack):
+    # fault.stop("notification-service"); crisis; sleep 120; fault.start; hop clinician_page: 1 page within 90 s of start, still 1 after 30 s more
+async def test_rejected_primary_recipient_pages_the_fallback_mailbox(e2e_stack):
+    # clinician new_email("reject") provisioned with assign=user.id (care team); crisis
+    # hop fallback_page: fallback-oncall@test.invalid gets 1 page for this user's episode within 60 s; reject address gets 0
+async def test_unreachable_recipient_for_one_user_does_not_delay_another_users_page(e2e_stack):
+    # crisis-review F1: user A's care-team clinician new_email("reject") (refused at RCPT) via assign=A.id; user B's care-team
+    # clinician new_email("clin") via assign=B.id; A sends a crisis, then B one second later
+    # hop clinician_page: B's episode page reaches B's clinician within 60 s of B's message; A's episode page reaches the
+    # fallback mailbox (pages_for_episode) and nothing reaches A's reject address; neither user waits on the other's retries
+@pytest.mark.parametrize("mode", ["stop", "pause"])
+async def test_postgres_outage_during_a_crisis_turn_still_pages_once_after_recovery(e2e_stack, mode):
+    # crisis-review F2. User and care-team clinician provisioned first;
+    # fault.stop or fault.pause("postgres"); crisis message; hop rest_crisis_object: crisis set within 3 s;
+    # after 60 s fault.start or fault.unpause("postgres") (also in finally); wait_ready for orchestrator, safety, notification
+    # hop clinician_page: within 120 s of recovery crisis_episodes(user) has exactly 1 episode and its page reaches the
+    # clinician; still 1 page per level 30 s later. From W2 the thread-ownership lookup (WS-11.7) also sits on this path and
+    # must stay inside the 3 s budget; this test guards it at every later barrier.
+async def test_kafka_down_at_boot_ready_503_then_recovery_and_delivery(e2e_stack):
+    # fault.stop("kafka"); restart orchestrator, safety, notification; hop ready_503: /ready 503 for each within 60 s
+    # fault.start("kafka"); hop ready_recovers: /ready 200 within 180 s; crisis -> hop clinician_page: 1 page
+@pytest.mark.parametrize("mode", ["error", "timeout"])
+async def test_llm_failure_still_gives_resources_and_a_page(e2e_stack, mode):
+    # llm.scenario(mode); hop rest_crisis_object (crisis within 3 s even in "timeout"); hop clinician_page
+async def test_safety_service_down_uses_local_screen_and_reports_degraded(e2e_stack):
+    # fault.stop("safety-service"); hop rest_crisis_object: crisis set and any(d.startswith("safety_service") for d in degraded)
+    # hop clinician_page: 1 page
+async def test_safety_service_hung_still_answers_within_3s(e2e_stack):
+    # crisis-review F7: fault.pause("safety-service") (a hung /check, not a refused connection); crisis message
+    # hop rest_crisis_object: elapsed < 3.0, crisis set and "safety_service_timeout" in degraded; unpause in finally
+    # hop clinician_page: 1 page
+async def test_orchestrator_and_safety_detecting_the_same_message_page_once(e2e_stack):
+    # hop clinician_page: 1 page per level over 60 s (normally exactly 1); crisis_episodes(user) has 1 row with >= 2 sources
+async def test_unacknowledged_episode_repages_backup_and_fallback(e2e_stack):
+    # primary on call + backup (backup=True); crisis; no ack
+    # hop repage: backup and fallback each get a page within 30 + 90 s; primary still has exactly 1
+```
+In `tests/e2e/test_w1_negative_phrases.py`:
+```python
+def test_e2e_phrases_are_in_the_clinical_phrase_set():
+    with hop("phrase_set"):
+        data = yaml.safe_load((REPO / "tests/clinical/crisis_phrases_v1.yaml").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
+        positives, negatives = {p["text"] for p in data["positive"]}, {n["text"] for n in data["negative"]}
+        assert set(CRISIS_PHRASES.values()) <= positives and set(BENIGN_PHRASES.values()) <= negatives
+
+@pytest.mark.parametrize("kind", sorted(BENIGN_PHRASES))
+async def test_benign_phrase_pages_nobody(e2e_stack, kind):
+    user = await make_user(e2e_stack)
+    with hop("benign_no_page"):
+        r = await e2e_stack.edge("POST", "/api/v1/orchestrator/chat", token=user.access_token, json={"message": BENIGN_PHRASES[kind]})
+        assert r.status_code == 200 and (r.json()["crisis"] is None or r.json()["crisis"]["level"] in ("NONE", "LOW", "ELEVATED"))
+        await asyncio.sleep(30)
+        assert crisis_episodes(e2e_stack, user.id) == []      # scoped to this user: other tests' re-pages share the mailboxes (F6)
+```
+- [ ] **Step 2: Run them and confirm they fail.** Run, one process each: E2E command (dev) with both files and `SOLACE_MANIFEST_IGNORE=1`; E2E command (strict, production). Expected: FAIL at `provision_clinician` or `rest_crisis_object` for every fault test, because W1 phase B (provisioning, crisis object, episodes) has not landed. `phrase_set` fails if `tests/clinical/crisis_phrases_v1.yaml` lacks the e2e phrases. The crisis-review tests (F1 isolation, F2 Postgres outage, F7 hung safety-service) fail at the same early hops in W0/W1 phase A and pin, from the W1 barrier on, that one user's unreachable recipient never delays another user's page, that a database outage during a crisis turn still pages once, and that a hung `/check` stays inside the 3 s budget.
+- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** the helpers and overlay values listed. Add one manifest entry per observed failing test id and hop: `target_wave: 1`, both profiles.
+- [ ] **Step 4: Run them and confirm they pass.** Run the two commands without `SOLACE_MANIFEST_IGNORE`. Expected: exit 0 with the listed tests XFAIL. At the W1 barrier (`SOLACE_BARRIER_WAVE=1`) both profiles must be fully green.
+- [ ] **Step 5: Regression.** Run the E2E command (dev) with `tests/e2e`. Expected: pass or XFAIL as listed.
+- [ ] **Step 6: Checkpoint:** `& D:\tmp\mvp-exec\tools\checkpoint.ps1 -Label "WS-05.8:done"` and record the printed tree id in the ledger. No git commit.

+**Covers:** MVP-039
+**Severity:** high
+**Wave:** 1
+**Depends on:** WS-05.8
+**Implementer model:** opus
+**Review:** safety-critical

+- Modify: `docker-compose.test.yml` (Alertmanager environment: `ALERTMANAGER_SMTP_SMARTHOST=mailpit:1025`, `ALERTMANAGER_EMAIL_TO=ops-alerts@test.invalid`, `ALERTMANAGER_EMAIL_FROM=ops-alertmanager@test.invalid`)

+**Interfaces:**
+- Consumes:
+  - IF-18 W1 rules: `CrisisAlertDeliveryFailed`, `CrisisOutboxBacklog`, `NotificationConsumerLag`, `DLQUnresolved`, `ServiceNotReady`, plus WS-04.7's `CrisisConsumerStalled` (crisis-review F13);
+  - IF-05 metrics (`solace_dlq_unresolved{group}` etc.; from WS-06.10 every event plane also samples the crisis group's `solace_consumer_lag`, so the series exists while notification-service is stopped or frozen);
+  - Alertmanager routing to `ALERTMANAGER_EMAIL_TO` (request 4).
+- Produces: the W1 alerting exit criterion.

+- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests** (`pytest.mark.timeout(1500)`; each starts with `with hop("alertmanager_running"): assert "alertmanager" in _running_services(e2e_stack)`):
+```python
+async def test_dlq_unresolved_alert_reaches_the_ops_mailbox(e2e_stack):
+    # e2e_stack.compose("exec","-T","kafka","bash","-c","printf 'not-json\n' | kafka-console-producer --bootstrap-server localhost:9092 --topic solace.safety")
+    # hop alert_email: wait_for_alert(e2e_stack, "DLQUnresolved", timeout=360)
+async def test_notification_stopped_fires_service_not_ready_and_consumer_lag(e2e_stack):
+    # fault.stop("notification-service"); send 11 crisis messages from 11 users
+    # hop alert_email: "ServiceNotReady" within 300 s and "NotificationConsumerLag" within 540 s; fault.start in finally
+async def test_outbox_backlog_fires_when_kafka_is_down(e2e_stack):
+    # fault.stop("kafka"); one crisis message; hop alert_email: "CrisisOutboxBacklog" within 360 s; fault.start in finally
+async def test_crisis_alert_delivery_failure_fires(e2e_stack):
+    # care-team clinician new_email("reject") via assign; crisis; hop alert_email: "CrisisAlertDeliveryFailed" within 360 s
+async def test_single_crisis_turn_with_a_stalled_consumer_fires_crisis_consumer_stalled(e2e_stack):
+    # crisis-review F13: fault.pause("notification-service") (alive container, no progress); ONE crisis message from one user
+    # (4 events on solace.safety, below NotificationConsumerLag's 10); hop alert_email: "CrisisConsumerStalled" within 420 s;
+    # fault.unpause in finally; hop clinician_page after unpause: that user's episode is paged within 120 s
+```
+- [ ] **Step 2: Run them and confirm they fail.** Run: E2E command (dev) with `tests/e2e/test_w1_crisis_alerting.py` and `SOLACE_MANIFEST_IGNORE=1`. Expected: FAIL at hop `alertmanager_running`, because the observability fragment with Alertmanager does not exist yet. Even with it, no rule could fire: there are no crisis-path metrics (MVP-039, with MVP-353 tested in WS-06.10). The stalled-consumer case also needs WS-06.10's crisis-group watch and WS-04.7's `CrisisConsumerStalled`; without them one stuck crisis turn (4 messages) alerts nobody (crisis-review F13).

+## Controller addendum (W1-prep, R-9)
+Set the Alertmanager environment in docker-compose.test.yml to the WS-04 request values, not the ones above: ALERTMANAGER_SMTP_SMARTHOST=mailpit:1025, ALERTMANAGER_EMAIL_TO=ops-alerts@test.invalid, ALERTMANAGER_EMAIL_FROM=alerts@test.invalid, ALERTMANAGER_SMTP_REQUIRE_TLS=false (mailpit has no TLS; WS-04.7's e2e accepts only these addresses). Dispatch after WS-05.8.
+

+<!-- brief extracted from WS-06-event-plane.md -->
+### Task WS-06.10: Backlog, lag and DLQ signals for the crisis pipeline
+**Covers:** MVP-353
+**Severity:** blocker
+**Wave:** 1
+**Depends on:** WS-06.9
+**Implementer model:** sonnet
+**Review:** safety-critical
+**Decision gate:** none
+**Real infra:** yes
+**Files:**
+- Create: `src/solace_events/metrics.py`. The prometheus_client objects on the default `REGISTRY`:
+  - `solace_outbox_pending{service}` (Gauge)
+  - `solace_outbox_oldest_pending_seconds{service}` (Gauge)
+  - `solace_consumer_lag{group,topic}` (Gauge)
+  - `solace_dlq_unresolved{group}` (Gauge)
+  - `solace_event_publish_failures_total{service}` (Counter)
+  - `solace_consumer_handler_failures_total{group,event_type}` (Counter)
+
+  Label values are service names, group ids, topics and event types only.
+- Modify: `src/solace_events/postgres_stores.py`: `PostgresOutboxStore.pending_stats() -> tuple[int, float]` counts `PENDING`/`PUBLISHING` rows for its own service and returns the age of the oldest in seconds (0.0 when none). `PostgresDLQStore.count_unresolved_by_group` is used as is.
+- Modify: `src/solace_events/publisher.py`: `InMemoryOutboxStore.pending_stats()`. `EventPublisher.publish` increments `solace_event_publish_failures_total` before raising `EventPublishError`.
+- Modify: `src/solace_events/consumer.py`:
+  - every failed handler attempt increments `solace_consumer_handler_failures_total`;
+  - `KafkaConsumerAdapter.lag() -> dict[str, int]` is implemented. AIOKafka computes it from broker state for every partition of each subscribed topic (topic metadata, not only this member's assignment), as end offset minus committed offset, a missing commit counting from the partition's earliest offset; so a member that holds no partition after a rebalance still reports the group's lag (crisis-review F13). The mock reports pending messages;
+  - the lag value is mirrored into `ConsumerMetrics.current_lag`.
+- Modify: `src/solace_events/service_plane.py`:
+  - add a sampler task, `metrics_interval_seconds` (default 15 s), that sets the four gauges for the plane's service and groups. A failed sample keeps the last value and logs one WARNING per minute;
+  - new constructor keyword `watch_lag: Sequence[tuple[str, str]] = CRISIS_LAG_WATCH`, with `CRISIS_LAG_WATCH = (("solace-group-notifications", "solace.safety"),)`: every plane's sampler also sets `solace_consumer_lag{group,topic}` for each watched pair from broker state through the admin client, whether or not this process consumes it, so the crisis consumer's lag stays visible while notification-service is down, frozen or holds no partition (crisis-review F13); a mock or disabled plane skips the watch;
+  - new `def record_publish_failure(service_name: str) -> None`: increments `solace_event_publish_failures_total{service=service_name}` for callers whose `publish(conn=)` transaction fails outside the plane (WS-12's R-12-2, crisis-review F11).
+- Test: `tests/solace_events/test_mvp_353_event_metrics.py` (new)
+- Test: `tests/realinfra/event-plane/test_mvp_353_lag_and_backlog.py` (new)
+
+**Interfaces:**
+- Consumes: IF-05 metric names (frozen); prometheus-client pin (WS-01).
+- Produces: the six IF-05 series. WS-04 builds alert rules on them: CrisisOutboxBacklog, NotificationConsumerLag, CrisisConsumerStalled (crisis-review F13), DLQUnresolved and CrisisEventPublishFailures. Because several planes export the watched crisis pair, rules over `solace_consumer_lag` aggregate with `max`, never `sum`. WS-05 drives those rules in its W1 alerting E2E. Also `record_publish_failure(service_name)` and `CRISIS_LAG_WATCH`; `record_publish_failure` is consumed by WS-12.9 (R-12-2) and by WS-10.3's publish retrier for timed-out attempts (crisis-review F2, F11).
+
+- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests.** In `tests/solace_events/test_mvp_353_event_metrics.py`:

+

+    # adapter.lag() == {"solace.safety": 2}

+In `tests/realinfra/event-plane/test_mvp_353_lag_and_backlog.py`:
+```python

+    # topic, group = unique names; produce 1 crisis event to topic; no consumer of that group ever runs
+    # plane = ServiceEventPlane(f"svc-f13-{tag}", pool=pg_migrated.app_pool, kafka_settings=..., strict=False,
+    #                           metrics_interval_seconds=1.0, watch_lag=((group, topic),)); no subscribe; start
+    # within 30 s: REGISTRY solace_consumer_lag{group=group, topic=topic} == 1; plane.stop() in finally
